**Regulatory Law No. 125(Ι)/2018.**  
With the previous law  
**Regulatory Law No. 23(Ι)/2022**, it was not amended  
to the specific provisions of Articles 99 - 106 of Law  
112(Ι)/2004. However, the amendments relate to the  
events that have occurred and have been memorialized by the  
authorities and are excluded from the provisions of the  
**Regulatory Law No. 23(Ι)/2022**, as well as the  
provisions that are included. In accordance with Article 98,  
the regulatory framework of Article 98 of Law  
2017 refers to the memorialization of the  
**Regulatory Law No. 184(Ι)/2017**, which  
provides for the regulatory framework of the  
**Regulatory Law No. 2020**. The core of the  
law is the regulatory framework of the  
**Regulatory Law No. 2020** and the  
**Regulatory Law No. 2020**. With the basis of  
Article 35, the regulatory framework of the  
**Regulatory Law** is applicable to the  
**Regulatory Law No. 2020**.  
**Regulatory Law No. 2020** was enacted on the  
**Regulatory Law No. 2002** and the  
**Regulatory Law No. 2022**, which refers to the  
accompanying report: 5 **Regulatory Framework**

**regulation of the operation of the**

**Supervision of the Implementation of**  
**Regulations concerning the**  
**Protection of Personal Data.**  
**Law 28(III)/2001.**  

**2(b) Council of Ministers for the**  
**regulation of the Protection of**  
**Personal Data concerning the**  
**processing of personal data by the**  
**National Authority for the**  
**Supervision of the Implementation of**  
**Regulations concerning the**  
**Protection of Personal Data.**  
**ETS No. 108.**  

**Law 2020, No. 6(II)/2020.**  
**Article 4 of Law 125(A)/2018**  
**provides for the establishment of**  
**an independent Authority for the**  
**Protection of Personal Data.**  

**3. Law 2016/680 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016**  
**on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data by**  
**competent authorities for the purposes of prevention, investigation, detection or**  
**prosecution of criminal offenses or the execution of criminal penalties, and**  
**on the free movement of such data.**  

**4. Law 2008/977/JHA.**  
**The provisions of the Law on the Protection of Personal Data apply to the**  
**processing of personal data in the context of the activities of the**  
**European Union Agency for Law Enforcement Cooperation (Europol).**  

**5. Law 2016/794 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 11 May 2016**  
**on the establishment of the European Agency for Law Enforcement Training (CEPOL)**  
**and repealing Decision 2005/681/JHA.**  

**6. Law 2018, No. 126(A)/2018.**  
**Article 15 of Law 125(A)/2018 provides for the establishment of an independent**  
**Authority for the Protection of Personal Data, which shall ensure compliance with**  
**the provisions of the law regarding the processing of personal data.**  

**7. Law 603/2013 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26 November 2013**  
**regarding the establishment of the Eurodac system for the comparison of**  
**fingerprints of applicants for international protection and for the purpose of**  
**the effective application of the Dublin Regulation.**

**401. Law 2013/603 regarding the processing of personal data in the context of the**

**402. Law 2013/604 regarding the processing of personal data in the context of the**  
**Eurodac system for the purpose of the effective application of the Dublin Regulation.**  

**403. Law 2013/603 regarding the processing of personal data in the context of
**Regulation on the Eurodac System for the Comparison of Fingerprints for the Purpose of Identifying Applicants for International Protection and for the Purpose of the Application of the Dublin Regulation (Regulation (EU) No. 1077/2011) concerning the implementation of the Regulation (EU) No. 603/2013, the European Agency for the Operational Management of Large-Scale IT Systems in the Area of Freedom, Security and Justice (eu-LISA), and the European Data Protection Supervisor (EDPS).**

1. **General Provisions**
   - This Regulation establishes the framework for the operation of the Eurodac system, which is designed to assist in the identification of applicants for international protection and to facilitate the application of the Dublin Regulation.

2. **Legal Basis**
   - The legal basis for this Regulation is provided by Article 30 of Regulation (EU) No. 603/2013, which outlines the objectives and functions of the Eurodac system.

3. **Implementation**
   - The implementation of the Eurodac system shall be carried out in accordance with the provisions of this Regulation and the applicable Union law.

4. **Data Protection**
   - The processing of personal data within the Eurodac system shall comply with the principles of data protection as set out in Regulation (EU) 2016/679 (General Data Protection Regulation) and Regulation (EU) 2018/1725 (on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data by the Union institutions, bodies, offices, and agencies).

5. **Amendments and Repeals**
   - This Regulation shall amend and repeal previous regulations and directives that are inconsistent with its provisions, including but not limited to Regulation (EC) No. 2725/2000 and Regulation (EU) No. 604/2013.

6. **Final Provisions**
   - This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. 

7. **Review and Reporting**
   - The Commission shall review the operation of the Eurodac system and report to the European Parliament and the Council every three years.

8. **Transitional Provisions**
   - The provisions of this Regulation shall apply to all data processed under the Eurodac system, including data collected prior to the entry into force of this Regulation.

9. **Entry into Force**
   - This Regulation shall be binding in its entirety and directly applicable in all Member States.

**Legal Document Translation**

**Regulation No. 143(A)/2021**  
**Regarding the Implementation of the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) 2016/679**  
**Published on 14th November 2018**  
**Concerning the Protection of Personal Data in the Context of the Free Movement of Such Data**  
**In accordance with the provisions of the Law on the Protection of Individuals with regard to the Processing of Personal Data (Law No. 138(I)/2001)**  
**Amended by Law No. 2(I)/2021.**  

1. The present regulation aims to ensure the protection of personal data and the privacy of individuals in the context of the processing of personal data.
2. The regulation applies to all entities that process personal data within the jurisdiction of the Republic of Cyprus.
3. It is essential that all processing of personal data is conducted in a lawful, fair, and transparent manner.

**Article 2: Definitions**  
For the purposes of this regulation:  
1. "Personal Data" means any information relating to an identified or identifiable natural person.  
2. "Processing" means any operation or set of operations which is performed on personal data, whether or not by automated means.  
3. "Data Subject" means the identified or identifiable natural person to whom the personal data relates.  

**Article 3: Principles of Processing Personal Data**  
1. Personal data shall be processed lawfully, fairly, and in a transparent manner in relation to the data subject.  
2. Personal data shall be collected for specified, legitimate purposes and not further processed in a manner that is incompatible with those purposes.  
3. Personal data shall be adequate, relevant, and limited to what is necessary in relation to the purposes for which they are processed.  

**Article 4: Rights of the Data Subject**  
1. The data subject has the right to access their personal data.  
2. The data subject has the right to rectification of inaccurate personal data.  
3. The data subject has the right to erasure of personal data.  

**Article 5: Obligations of the Data Controller**  
1. The data controller shall implement appropriate technical and organizational measures to ensure and to be able to demonstrate that processing is performed in accordance with this regulation.  
2. The data controller shall maintain a record of processing activities under its responsibility.  

**Article 6: Enforcement and Penalties**  
1. Any violation of the provisions of this regulation may result in administrative fines.  
2. The supervisory authority shall have the power to impose fines and to take corrective measures.  

**Final Provisions**  
This regulation shall enter into force on the 28th of November 2018.  

**Regulation No. 1860**  
**Published on 28th November 2018**

**Article 1: General Provisions**

1.1. The present regulation shall apply to the provisions of the Regulation (EU) 2016/679 regarding the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data and on the free movement of such data.

1.2. The processing of personal data shall be lawful only if and to the extent that at least one of the following applies:
   a) The data subject has given consent to the processing of his or her personal data for one or more specific purposes;
   b) Processing is necessary for the performance of a contract to which the data subject is party or in order to take steps at the request of the data subject prior to entering into a contract;
   c) Processing is necessary for compliance with a legal obligation to which the controller is subject;
   d) Processing is necessary in order to protect the vital interests of the data subject or of another natural person;
   e) Processing is necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest or in the exercise of official authority vested in the controller;
   f) Processing is necessary for the purposes of the legitimate interests pursued by the controller or by a third party, except where such interests are overridden by the interests or fundamental rights and freedoms of the data subject.

1.3. The data subject shall have the right to withdraw his or her consent at any time. The withdrawal of consent shall not affect the lawfulness of processing based on consent before its withdrawal.

1.4. The controller shall implement appropriate technical and organizational measures to ensure and to be able to demonstrate that processing is performed in accordance with this Regulation.

1.5. The controller shall be responsible for, and be able to demonstrate compliance with, paragraph 1.4.

**Article 2: Rights of the Data Subject**

2.1. The data subject shall have the right to access his or her personal data and to obtain from the controller confirmation as to whether or not personal data concerning him or her are being processed.

2.2. The data subject shall have the right to request rectification of inaccurate personal data concerning him or her without undue delay.

2.3. The data subject shall have the right to request the erasure of personal data concerning him or her without undue delay and the controller shall be obliged to erase personal data without undue delay where one of the following grounds applies:
   a) The personal data are no longer necessary in relation to the purposes for which they were collected or otherwise processed;
   b) The data subject withdraws consent on which the processing is based and where there is no other legal ground for the processing;
   c) The data subject objects to the processing and there are no overriding legitimate grounds for the processing;
   d) The personal data have been unlawfully processed;
   e) The personal data have to be erased for compliance with a legal obligation in Union or Member State law to which the controller is subject.

2.4. The data subject shall have the right to data portability, which allows him or her to receive the personal data concerning him or her, which he or she has provided to a controller, in a structured, commonly used and machine-readable format and has the right to transmit those data to another controller without hindrance from the controller to which the personal data have been provided.

**Article 3: Obligations of the Controller**

3.1. The controller shall implement appropriate technical and organizational measures to ensure a level of security appropriate to the risk, including, as appropriate:
   a) The pseudonymization and encryption of personal data;
   b) The ability to ensure the ongoing confidentiality, integrity, availability and resilience of processing systems and services;
   c) The ability to restore the availability and access to personal data in a timely manner in the event of a physical or technical incident;
   d) A process for regularly testing, assessing and evaluating the effectiveness of technical and organizational measures for ensuring the security of the processing.

3.2. The controller shall ensure that any natural person acting under the authority of the controller and who has access to personal data is obliged to maintain confidentiality.

3.3. The controller shall notify the supervisory authority of a personal data breach without undue delay and, where feasible, not later than 72 hours after having become aware of it, unless the personal data breach is unlikely to result in a risk to the rights and freedoms of natural persons.

3.4. The controller shall communicate the personal data breach to the data subject without undue delay when the personal data breach is likely to result in a high risk to the rights and freedoms of natural persons.

3.5. The controller shall maintain a record of processing activities under its responsibility, containing:
   a) The name and contact details of the controller and, where applicable, the joint controller, the representative of the controller and the data protection officer;
   b) The purposes of the processing;
   c) A description of the categories of data subjects and of the categories of personal data;
   d) The categories of recipients to whom the personal data have been or will be disclosed;
   e) Where applicable, transfers of personal data to a third country or an international organization, including the identification of that third country or international organization;
   f) The time limits for erasure of the different categories of data;
   g) A general description of the technical and organizational security measures.

**Article 4: Final Provisions**

4.1. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union.

4.2. This Regulation shall be binding in its entirety and directly applicable in all Member States.

**Inheritance Regulations**

1. The inheritance shall be governed by the provisions of the applicable law. However, if the deceased has made a will, the provisions of the will shall prevail. 

2. If the deceased has not made a will, the inheritance shall be distributed according to the rules of intestate succession, as provided by the applicable law. 

3. The inheritance shall not be subject to any restrictions. However, the rights of the heirs shall be subject to the provisions of the applicable law regarding the division of the estate.

3.1.29. The inheritance for any incapacity claim shall be governed by the provisions of AAA TRADE LTD. The claim shall be submitted to the applicable law regarding the incapacity claim of AAA TRADE LTD, which shall be processed according to the provisions of the applicable law.

3.1.30. The inheritance for any incapacity claim shall be governed by the provisions of VPR Safe Financial Group Limited. The claim shall be submitted to the applicable law regarding the incapacity claim of VPR Safe Financial Group Limited. 

4. The applicable law shall determine the rights of the heirs regarding the distribution of the estate, and the heirs shall be entitled to receive their respective shares as per the applicable law.

5. The provisions of the applicable law shall apply to any disputes arising from the inheritance, and the heirs shall have the right to seek legal recourse in accordance with the applicable law.

6. In the event of any disputes, the heirs may seek resolution through mediation or arbitration, as provided by the applicable law.

7. The applicable law shall govern the validity of any agreements made by the heirs regarding the distribution of the estate.

8. The heirs shall be responsible for any debts of the deceased, and the estate shall be liable for the payment of such debts as per the applicable law.

9. The provisions of the applicable law shall apply to the enforcement of any claims related to the inheritance.

10. The heirs shall have the right to contest any claims made against the estate, and such contests shall be governed by the applicable law.

**2. Definitions**  
For the purposes of this document, the term "Electronic Contract" refers to the electronic agreement made between the parties involved, which is governed by the specific laws applicable to the jurisdiction of the contract.

**3. Specific Provisions**

The parties agree that the electronic contract shall be governed by the laws of the jurisdiction where the service provider operates, specifically the jurisdiction of the broker NessFX (www.nessfx.com), which is the electronic service provider. 1.1. The provisions of this document shall apply to the parties involved, but also with regard to third parties.  
1.2. Specifically, unless otherwise stated, the provisions herein shall apply to the following:  
   a. The parties involved in the agreement, and  
   b. The parties involved in the agreement concerning the provisions of the applicable law (the provisions concerning the rights of the parties involved in the agreement).  
1.3. In the event that the parties involved do not comply with the provisions, they shall be liable to the other party for any damages incurred.  
1.4. The parties involved shall be required to provide any necessary documentation to the relevant authorities.  
1.5. The parties involved shall ensure that they comply with the applicable laws and regulations, including but not limited to the rights of third parties.  
1.6. The parties involved shall be liable for any damages caused by their non-compliance with the provisions of this document.  
1.7. The parties involved shall be required to notify the relevant authorities of any changes to their circumstances that may affect their obligations under this document.

**Section 2: Specific Provisions**

2.1. The parties involved shall be responsible for ensuring that any agreements made are in accordance with the applicable laws and regulations.  
2.2. The parties involved shall be required to maintain accurate records of their transactions and activities.

2.4. The parties involved shall be required to cooperate with any investigations conducted by the relevant authorities.

**Section 3: Final Provisions**

3.1. This document shall be governed by the laws of the relevant jurisdiction.  
3.2. Any disputes arising from this document shall be resolved in accordance with the applicable laws and regulations.  
3.3. The parties involved shall be required to comply with any decisions made by the relevant authorities.  
3.4. This document may be amended or modified only by written agreement of the parties involved.  
3.5. The parties involved shall be required to notify each other of any changes to their contact information.

**Section 4: Miscellaneous**

4.1. This document constitutes the entire agreement between the parties involved and supersedes any prior agreements or understandings.  
4.2. If any provision of this document is found to be invalid or unenforceable, the remaining provisions shall remain in full force and effect.  
4.3. The parties involved acknowledge that they have read and understood the provisions of this document and agree to be bound by its terms.  
4.4. This document may be executed in counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an original, but all of which together shall constitute one and the same instrument.

**1. General Provisions**

1.1. The provisions of this document shall not be construed to include any provisions that may contradict the applicable law. The provisions shall be interpreted in accordance with the applicable law.

1.2. The parties, in their capacity as representatives of the parties involved, shall provide assistance in accordance with the applicable law, ensuring that the provisions are not in conflict with the law and do not violate the rights of any third party.

1.3. In the event that the provisions are deemed invalid or unenforceable, the remaining provisions shall remain in effect, and the parties shall negotiate in good faith to replace the invalid provisions with valid provisions that reflect the original intent of the parties.

**2. Confidentiality**

2.1. The parties agree that any confidential information exchanged between them shall be treated as confidential and shall not be disclosed to any third party without the prior written consent of the disclosing party.

2.2. The obligation of confidentiality shall survive the termination of this agreement and shall remain in effect for a period of five (5) years from the date of termination.

**3. Governing Law and Jurisdiction**

**3.3.5. Declaration for the Application of Provisional Measures**

The provisional measures requested by the applicant, Mr. Nera Mirolo, are aimed at ensuring the protection of his rights, specifically regarding the preservation of the property in question, which is a residential property located in the area of Nera Mirolo. The applicant, in particular, seeks to prevent any actions that may affect the ownership of the property, including the transfer or encumbrance of said property, in order to maintain the status quo until a final decision is made regarding the merits of the case. 

The applicant asserts that the requested provisional measures are necessary to prevent irreparable harm to his rights and to ensure that he can effectively pursue his claims in the main proceedings. The applicant further argues that the urgency of the situation justifies the immediate granting of these measures, as any delay could result in significant detriment to his legal position.

**3.3.6. Declaration for the Application of Provisional Measures**

The provisional measures requested by the applicant, regarding the insurance coverage provided by CNP ASFALISTIKI LIMITED, are aimed at ensuring that the applicant's rights are protected during the ongoing proceedings. The applicant requests that the court order the immediate provision of insurance coverage for the property in question, which is essential for safeguarding the applicant's interests while the case is pending. 

The applicant emphasizes that the requested measures are necessary to prevent any potential loss or damage to the property, which could undermine the effectiveness of the final judgment. The applicant also highlights that the insurance coverage is crucial for maintaining the value of the property and ensuring that any risks associated with it are adequately managed.

**3.3.7. Declaration for the Application of Provisional Measures**

The provisional measures requested by the applicant regarding the insurance coverage provided by Gan Direct Insurance Limited are aimed at ensuring that the applicant's rights are protected during the ongoing proceedings. The applicant requests that the court order the immediate provision of insurance coverage for the property in question, which is essential for safeguarding the applicant's interests while the case is pending. 

The applicant asserts that the requested measures are necessary to prevent any potential loss or damage to the property, which could undermine the effectiveness of the final judgment. The applicant further argues that the insurance coverage is crucial for maintaining the value of the property and ensuring that any risks associated with it are adequately managed. 

In conclusion, the applicant requests that the court grant the provisional measures as outlined above, as they are essential for the protection of his rights and interests during the course of the proceedings.

**92 23 Provisional Measures**  
Law 125(A)/2018, under the provisions of the applicable regulations, the following measures are established:  
1. (a) It is provided that the competent authority shall issue and enforce regulations regarding the collection of taxes in accordance with the provisions of Article 14.  
(b) It shall be prohibited and shall be demonstrated that the collection of taxes in accordance with the provisions of Article 14 is being carried out.  
(c) It shall be included that the relevant authorities, whether administrative or judicial, shall be informed regarding the collection of taxes in accordance with the provisions of Article 14.  
(d) It shall be conducted with the necessary controls.  
(e) It is required that the competent authority shall ensure that the collection of taxes is conducted in accordance with the applicable laws and regulations, and that the necessary measures are taken for the enforcement of the relevant provisions.

93  (β) Enforcement· (γ) Enforcement
for the enforcement of the
provisions, if applicable,
in accordance with the provisions of the law· (δ) Provisional
or ordinary judicial measures for the
protection of rights· (ε) Specific
provisional measures for the
protection of rights (up to €200,000).» If the provisions
of (β), (γ), (δ), and (ε) are not
sufficiently clear, they shall be interpreted
in accordance with the applicable
provisions of the relevant legislation. 4.3. i-Justice
As of 2022, the operation of the
i-Justice system commenced. For the
operation of the i-Justice system, the
competent authority shall be the
Ministry of Justice, which shall
be responsible for the management of
the relevant system. 4.4. The
competent authority shall ensure that
the provisions of the relevant
legislation are applied in the
implementation of the European Travel Information and Authorisation System (ETIAS)
in the context of the implementation of the
legislation on the management of
the relevant system. The
management of the relevant system shall
be conducted in accordance with the
provisions of the applicable law, which
shall ensure that the rights of
individuals are protected in accordance with
the relevant provisions of the law. 4.5.
The competent authority shall be responsible for the
conduct of the electoral process
in accordance with the provisions of the
applicable electoral legislation. The
competent authority shall ensure that
the electoral process is conducted
in accordance with the principles of
transparency and fairness, and shall
be responsible for the oversight of
the electoral process, ensuring that
the rights of voters are protected
in accordance with the relevant provisions of the law. The
provisions of the applicable law shall
apply to the electoral process, which
shall be conducted in accordance with the
principles of legality and
non-discrimination, ensuring that
the rights of individuals are protected
in accordance with the relevant provisions of the law.
94  Summary with regard to the
Article 3 of Article 23
of the Administrative Procedure Code
regarding the electoral process,
is stated as follows: "The process of
electoral selection and the electoral
procedure shall be conducted in a
transparent manner, in order to ensure
the fair conduct of elections, to be
carried out with the utmost respect
for the principles of democracy." In Article 6(1)
of the Regulation, regarding the
legitimacy of the electoral process, it is stated: "1. The electoral process is deemed
legitimate if it is conducted in accordance with the
provisions of the applicable electoral law,

95  4.6.  Judicial Review of Administrative Decisions
The judicial review of administrative decisions is governed by the provisions of the Administrative Procedure Code. The review shall be conducted in accordance with the principles of legality and proportionality, ensuring that the administrative acts are lawful and do not infringe upon the rights of individuals. The court shall assess whether the administrative decision is justified based on the facts and the applicable law, and whether it respects the rights of the parties involved. 

4.7.  Appeal
The appeal process for the administrative decisions shall be in accordance with the provisions of Article 61(4)(a) and (b) of the Administrative Procedure Code and shall be subject to the provisions of the Law on Administrative Justice. The appeal must be filed within the time limits established by law, and the court shall examine the legality of the administrative decision in question. 

The provisions of the law shall apply to all cases where the administrative decision affects the rights of individuals, and the court shall ensure that the rights of the parties are protected. The appeal shall be based on the grounds of illegality, and the court shall have the authority to annul the administrative decision if it finds it to be unlawful. 

The administrative authority must provide a reasoned decision, and the parties shall have the right to be heard. The court shall ensure that the principles of fairness and justice are upheld in the review process. 

The legal framework governing the appeal process is designed to ensure that individuals have access to justice and that their rights are protected in accordance with the law. The court shall have the authority to issue interim measures if necessary to protect the rights of the parties during the appeal process. 

The appeal shall be filed with the competent administrative court, and the parties shall be notified of the proceedings. The court shall issue a decision based on the merits of the case, and the parties shall have the right to appeal the decision in accordance with the law. 

The administrative authority must comply with the court's decision, and failure to do so may result in legal consequences. The court shall ensure that the administrative authority acts in accordance with the law and respects the rights of individuals. 

The provisions of the law shall apply equally to all parties involved in the administrative proceedings, and the court shall ensure that the principles of equality and non-discrimination are upheld. 

The appeal process is an essential mechanism for ensuring accountability and transparency in administrative decision-making, and it provides individuals with a means to challenge unlawful administrative acts. The court shall ensure that the appeal process is conducted in a fair and impartial manner, and that the rights of all parties are respected throughout the proceedings.

99  "4.-(1)(α) It is proposed
Advisory Committee on
"Advisory Committee on
Administrative Matters,
Regarding the Advisory Committee and
Regulatory Affairs
Regarding the Advisory Committee", which is
authorized for- (i) the
registration of
administrative matters and
regulatory affairs concerning the
residential area of
Mihra, in relation to
the regulatory framework, (ii) the
provision of licenses for
industrial and agricultural
activities concerning the
residential area of Mihra, (iii) the
establishment of
regulatory measures for
the residential area of Mihra. (Ⅶ) 7.
The Committee, with
the necessary expertise regarding the
Regulatory Framework,
determines the
residential area of Mihra, in
relation to the
provisions of the Health
Regulatory Framework, in order to
ensure the proper
implementation and to
select the appropriate
measures for the
residential area of Mihra. (Ⅶ) 32. The
provisions of the
Regulatory Framework
Regarding the Advisory Committee and
Administrative Matters, shall
apply to the
registered administrative matters and
regulatory affairs concerning the
residential area of Mihra, provided
that the
registered administrative matters
are in accordance with the
provisions of the
Regulatory Framework, and

100  4.14.  Summary Report  
International Assessment Results PISA, TIMSS, and TALIS  
The summary report of the International Assessment Results PISA, TIMSS, and TALIS must be prepared in accordance with the legal framework established by the Regulatory Council, which is governed by the provisions of Article 7 of the Ministerial Decision No. 125(A)/2018, specifically regarding the following: "7. The summary report must include the following elements: (a) the summary of the results as per Article 5, and (b) the summary of the results as per Article 6 of the Regulation." The Regulatory Authority for Education, which is responsible for the preparation of the summary report, must ensure that the necessary legal provisions are adhered to, as outlined in Article 7 of the Ministerial Decision No. 125(A)/2018. The summary report must include the necessary information regarding the educational performance of students in the international assessments PISA, TIMSS, and TALIS, and must be prepared in a manner that is consistent with the requirements of the relevant legal framework. The Regulatory Authority for Education must ensure that the summary report is comprehensive and includes all relevant data, while also adhering to the principles of transparency and accountability in the educational process. The summary report must be submitted to the relevant authorities for review and approval, and must be made available to the public in accordance with the applicable laws and regulations. The summary report must also include any recommendations for improving educational outcomes based on the findings of the assessments. The Regulatory Authority for Education must ensure that the summary report is prepared in a timely manner and that it meets all necessary legal and regulatory requirements. The summary report must be disseminated to all stakeholders involved in the educational process, including schools, teachers, and parents, to ensure that they are informed of the results and can take appropriate action based on the findings.

**Legal Notice**

**Subject: Legal Proceedings Against the Company**

1. The undersigned, acting on behalf of the company, hereby submits a formal complaint regarding the actions of the defendant, which have caused significant harm to the company’s interests.

2. The defendant has engaged in activities that are detrimental to the company, including but not limited to the unauthorized use of proprietary information and breach of contractual obligations.

3. The plaintiff seeks redress for the damages incurred as a result of the defendant's actions, which have resulted in financial losses and reputational harm to the company.

4. The plaintiff requests that the court orders the defendant to cease and desist from further actions that infringe upon the company’s rights and to compensate the plaintiff for the losses sustained.

5. The plaintiff reserves the right to pursue additional legal remedies as deemed necessary, including but not limited to injunctive relief and recovery of attorney's fees.

6. This complaint is filed in accordance with the applicable laws and regulations governing such matters, and the plaintiff requests a hearing at the earliest convenience.

112  5.14.  Notification for
the submission of applications for the issuance of a
medical certificate for the purpose of
the National Health Organization (N.H.O.)
is hereby established as follows: (a) the applicant must submit a request for the issuance of a medical certificate to the relevant authority, (b) the request must be accompanied by the necessary documentation, including but not limited to identification documents, proof of residence, and any other relevant documents required by the applicable regulations. In the case of the submission of a request for the issuance of a medical certificate, the relevant authority shall examine the request and may request additional information or documentation as needed to process the application. The relevant authority shall issue the medical certificate within a reasonable time frame, provided that all necessary documentation has been submitted and is in order.

**Agreement between Hermes, S.A. and Dynamic Works LIMITED**

This agreement is made between Hermes, S.A. (hereinafter referred to as "Hermes") and Dynamic Works LIMITED (hereinafter referred to as "Dynamic Works"). The purpose of this agreement is to outline the terms and conditions under which the parties will engage in the activities specified herein.

1. **General Provisions**
   - The parties agree to collaborate on the development of projects, as specified in Article 1, which shall be governed by the terms of this agreement.
   - The agreement shall be binding and enforceable in accordance with the applicable laws and regulations.

2. **Obligations of the Parties**
   - Hermes shall provide the necessary resources and support for the execution of the projects.
   - Dynamic Works shall ensure that all deliverables meet the quality standards agreed upon by both parties.

3. **Compensation**
   - a) Hermes shall pay Dynamic Works a fee of €6,000 for the services rendered under Article 1.
   - b) For the services rendered under Article 2, Hermes shall compensate Dynamic Works as follows:
     i) A payment of €5,000 for the completion of the project.
     ii) Additional payments may be made based on the performance and results achieved.

4. **Confidentiality**
   - Both parties agree to maintain the confidentiality of all proprietary information exchanged during the term of this agreement.
   - This obligation shall survive the termination of the agreement.

5. **Termination**
   - This agreement may be terminated by either party with written notice of 30 days.
   - Upon termination, all obligations under this agreement shall cease, except for those obligations that are expressly stated to survive termination.

6. **Governing Law**
   - This agreement shall be governed by and construed in accordance with the laws of the jurisdiction in which Hermes is incorporated.

7. **Dispute Resolution**
   - Any disputes arising out of or in connection with this agreement shall be resolved through mediation, and if unsuccessful, through arbitration in accordance with the rules of the relevant arbitration institution.

8. **Miscellaneous**
   - This agreement constitutes the entire understanding between the parties and supersedes all prior agreements and understandings, whether written or oral.
   - Any amendments to this agreement must be made in writing and signed by both parties.

In witness whereof, the parties have executed this agreement as of the date first above written.

**Hermes, S.A.**  
**Dynamic Works LIMITED**

Date: __________

**Community Guidelines of FACEBOOK,**

**Regarding the Reporting of Violations,**

This document serves to inform users about the rules, regulations, and procedures regarding the reporting of content that violates the community standards of FACEBOOK. In the event that a user encounters content that they believe violates these standards, they are encouraged to report it through the appropriate channels provided by FACEBOOK.

1. **Reporting Mechanism**  
   Users can report content by using the reporting feature available on the platform. This includes reporting posts, comments, or messages that are deemed inappropriate or violate community standards.

2. **Content Types for Reporting**  
   Users may report various types of content, including but not limited to hate speech, harassment, misinformation, and graphic violence. Each report will be reviewed by FACEBOOK's moderation team.

3. **Anonymity of Reports**  
   Reports made by users will remain anonymous. The identity of the reporting user will not be disclosed to the user whose content is being reported.

4. **Review Process**  
   Once a report is submitted, it will be reviewed by FACEBOOK's team to determine if the content violates community standards. Users will be notified of the outcome of their report.

5. **Consequences of Violations**  
   If content is found to violate community standards, appropriate actions will be taken, which may include the removal of the content, suspension of the account, or other penalties as deemed necessary.

6. **Changes to Reporting Guidelines**  
   FACEBOOK reserves the right to modify these reporting guidelines at any time. Users are encouraged to review the guidelines periodically to stay informed of any changes.

7. **Feedback and Appeals**  
   Users who disagree with the outcome of a report may provide feedback or appeal the decision through the designated channels on the platform.

8. **Contact Information**  
   For further inquiries or assistance regarding reporting violations, users may contact FACEBOOK's support team.

**Effective Date: November 16, 2022**  
**Published by: FACEBOOK**  
**Community Standards Team**  

This document outlines the procedures and expectations for users regarding the reporting of violations on the FACEBOOK platform. Users are encouraged to engage responsibly and report any content that they believe undermines the community standards.

**Legal Document - Translation**

**Subject: Legal Framework for the Protection of Personal Data**

**1. Introduction**
This document outlines the legal framework regarding the protection of personal data in the context of the European Union and Greece for the year 2022.

**2. Legislative Framework**
- Regulation (EU) 2016/679 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the protection of natural persons with regard to the processing of personal data and on the free movement of such data (General Data Protection Regulation - GDPR).
- Law 4624/2019 on the protection of personal data and the establishment of the Hellenic Data Protection Authority.

**3. Objectives**
The objectives of this legal framework include:
   1. Ensuring the protection of individuals' fundamental rights and freedoms, particularly their right to privacy.
   2. Establishing clear rules for the processing of personal data to enhance transparency and accountability.
   3. Promoting the free movement of personal data within the EU while ensuring a high level of protection.

**4. Scope of Application**
This legal framework applies to:
   1. All entities processing personal data within the EU, regardless of their location.
   2. Organizations outside the EU that offer goods or services to individuals within the EU or monitor their behavior.

**5. Key Principles**
The processing of personal data must adhere to the following principles:
   1. Lawfulness, fairness, and transparency.
   2. Purpose limitation.
   3. Data minimization.
   4. Accuracy.
   5. Storage limitation.
   6. Integrity and confidentiality.

**6. Rights of Data Subjects**
Data subjects have the following rights:
   1. The right to access their personal data.
   2. The right to rectification.
   3. The right to erasure (right to be forgotten).
   4. The right to restrict processing.
   5. The right to data portability.
   6. The right to object.

**7. Obligations of Data Controllers and Processors**
Data controllers and processors must:
   1. Implement appropriate technical and organizational measures to ensure and demonstrate compliance.
   2. Maintain records of processing activities.
   3. Notify the supervisory authority and affected individuals in the event of a data breach.

**8. Supervisory Authority**
The Hellenic Data Protection Authority is responsible for overseeing the application of this legal framework, providing guidance, and enforcing compliance.

**9. Conclusion**
This legal framework aims to protect the personal data of individuals while facilitating the free movement of such data within the EU. Compliance with these regulations is essential for maintaining trust and safeguarding privacy rights.

**10. References**
- Regulation (EU) 2016/679
- Law 4624/2019

**Date: 2022**  
**Prepared by: [Name/Organization]**  
**Contact Information: [Contact Details]**  

**End of Document**

10.1.2. Summary of the EDPB's activities regarding the implementation of the GDPR: 
The EDPB has been actively engaged in providing guidance and recommendations on various topics related to the application of the GDPR. The EDPB has also been involved in the examination of specific cases and has issued opinions and decisions to ensure consistent application of the regulation across Member States. 

The EDPB has also addressed issues related to the processing of personal data in the context of cross-border data transfers and has provided recommendations on how to ensure compliance with the GDPR in these situations. 

The EDPB has been working closely with national supervisory authorities to ensure effective enforcement of the GDPR and to facilitate cooperation among Member States.

152 10.1.3. Στρατηγική Δράση προς την ΕΣΠΕ για την προώθηση της προστασίας δεδομένων. Το Συμβούλιο της ΕΣΠΕ, στις 15 Φεβρουαρίου 2022, για την προώθηση της προστασίας δεδομένων. Σκοπός της στρατηγικής δράσης είναι η προώθηση της προστασίας δεδομένων και η αξιολόγηση της εφαρμογής της στην ΕΣΠΕ. Στρατηγική δράση περιλαμβάνει την αναγνώριση των δικαιωμάτων των πολιτών και την ενίσχυση της διαφάνειας και της λογοδοσίας. Η στρατηγική δράση θα περιλαμβάνει επίσης την ανάπτυξη και την εφαρμογή πολιτικών που θα διασφαλίσουν την ηλεκτρονική διακυβέρνηση και την προστασία των δεδομένων. Οι δράσεις αυτές θα είναι συμβατές με τις αρχές της ΕΣΠΕ και θα προωθήσουν την προστασία των δεδομένων στην ΕΣΠΕ. 10.2. Αρμοδιότητες εθνικών αρχών προστασίας δεδομένων.
**Legal Document Translation**

**Exchange of Personal Data**  
for the purpose of the operational  
implementation of the  
European Union Agency for Law Enforcement  
and the European Union Agency for Criminal Justice.  
The provisions shall apply  
to the exchange of personal data,  
as well as to the processing of personal data,  
in accordance with the General Data Protection Regulation  
and the relevant national laws. The  
provisions shall also apply  
to the processing of personal data  
in accordance with the relevant  
national laws of the Member States.  

**1. Purpose of the Exchange**  
The purpose of the exchange of personal data  
is to facilitate cooperation between  
the law enforcement authorities of the Member States  
in the fight against serious crime and terrorism.  
The exchange of personal data  
shall be conducted in accordance with  
the applicable legal framework,  
including the General Data Protection Regulation  
and national legislation.  

**2. Legal Basis**  
The legal basis for the exchange of personal data  
is provided by Article 45 of Regulation  
(EC) No 2016/794 (Europol Regulation),  
with specific reference to the  
General Data Protection Regulation.  

**3. Cooperation with Other Agencies**  
Europol shall cooperate with other relevant agencies,  
including Eurojust and OLAF,  
to ensure the effective exchange of personal data  
for the purposes of combating serious crime  
and terrorism.  

**4. Data Protection Principles**  
The processing of personal data  
shall be subject to the principles of necessity,  
proportionality, and legality,  
and shall be conducted in a manner  
that respects the rights of individuals.  

**5. Data Retention**  
Personal data exchanged shall be retained  
only for as long as necessary  
for the purposes for which it was collected,  
and shall be deleted when no longer needed.  

**6. Rights of Individuals**  
Individuals whose personal data are processed  
shall have the right to access their data,  
to request rectification, and to seek  
erasure of their data in accordance with  
the applicable legal framework.  

**7. Final Provisions**  
This agreement shall enter into force  
upon signature by the authorized representatives  
of the parties involved.  

**8. Amendments**  
Any amendments to this agreement  
shall be made in writing and signed  
by the authorized representatives  
of the parties involved.  

**9. Governing Law**  
This agreement shall be governed by  
the laws of the European Union and  
the national laws of the Member States.  

**10. Dispute Resolution**  
Any disputes arising from this agreement  
shall be resolved through negotiation  
between the parties involved.  

**11. Contact Information**  
For any inquiries regarding this agreement,  
the contact information of the parties  
shall be provided in an annex.  

**12. Signatures**  
The undersigned, being duly authorized,  
have signed this agreement on the date indicated below.  

**Date:**  
**Place:**  
**Signatures:**  
**For Europol:**  
**For Eurojust:**  
**For OLAF:**
**Agreement on the Eurodac System. SIS II**

**General Provisions**  
The Schengen Information System - SIS II regulates the exchange of information between Member States regarding the management of asylum applications and the control of irregular migrants, as well as for the prevention of serious criminal offenses and for the enforcement of the law. In this context, it is necessary to ensure that all relevant information is shared for the effective management of asylum applications and for the prevention of serious criminal offenses. The SIS II does not operate in isolation from the other systems. The Agreement must be interpreted in accordance with the provisions of the Schengen acquis, including the relevant regulations and directives. 

**Article 1**  
This Agreement establishes the legal framework for the exchange of information between Member States through the Schengen Information System II (SIS II) with respect to the management of asylum applications and the control of irregular migrants.

**Article 2**  
The Visa Information System (VIS) regulates the exchange of information between Member States regarding the management of visa applications and the control of irregular migrants. The VIS is intended to facilitate the processing of visa applications and to enhance the security of the Schengen area. 

**Article 3**  
The national VIS shall be connected to the central VIS and the national authorities shall ensure that the exchange of information is conducted in accordance with the applicable laws and regulations. The national VIS shall not be used for purposes other than those specified in this Agreement.

**Article 4**  
The Customs Information System (CIS) regulates the exchange of information between Member States regarding customs matters, including the management of customs controls and the prevention of customs offenses. The CIS is intended to enhance the effectiveness of customs cooperation and to ensure compliance with customs regulations.

**Article 5**  
The national authorities shall ensure that the exchange of information through the CIS is conducted in accordance with the applicable laws and regulations. The CIS shall not be used for purposes other than those specified in this Agreement.

**Article 6**  
The Schengen Member States shall ensure that the exchange of information through the SIS II, VIS, and CIS is conducted in a manner that respects the rights of individuals and complies with data protection regulations.

**Article 7**  
This Agreement shall enter into force on the date of its signature and shall remain in effect until terminated by mutual consent of the parties.

**Done in Brussels, on [date], in [number] copies, each in the languages of the parties.**
**Cooperation with the Internal Market Information System (IMI)**

**1. Introduction**  
The Internal Market Information System (IMI) is a tool that facilitates the exchange of information between national authorities in the European Union. It aims to improve the implementation of EU law and enhance cooperation among Member States.

**2. Objectives**  
The objectives of the IMI include:  
1. To support the effective application of EU legislation.  
2. To enable national authorities to communicate and share information efficiently.  
3. To assist in the resolution of cross-border issues related to the internal market.

**3. Legal Framework**  
The IMI operates under the legal framework established by the relevant EU regulations, including Regulation (EU) 2018/1724, which aims to simplify access to information and services for citizens and businesses.

**4. Responsibilities of National Authorities**  
National authorities are responsible for:  
1. Registering in the IMI system.  
2. Ensuring the accuracy and completeness of the information shared.  
3. Responding to requests for information from other Member States in a timely manner.

**5. Data Protection**  
All data exchanged through the IMI must comply with the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) to ensure the protection of personal data.

**6. Conclusion**  
The IMI is a vital tool for enhancing cooperation among Member States and ensuring the effective implementation of EU law. National authorities must actively participate and adhere to the established guidelines to maximize its benefits.
**Privacy Policy**

**Effective Date: 02/11/2022**

**Subject: Personal Data Protection**

**Article 1: General Provisions**

This Privacy Policy outlines the terms under which the company "Privacy Policy for Personal Data Protection" processes personal data, in accordance with the applicable legislation on the protection of personal data, including the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) and the relevant national laws.

**Article 2: Purpose of Data Processing**

The purpose of processing personal data is to ensure the proper functioning of the services provided by the company, including but not limited to:

1. **Communication**: To communicate with users regarding their inquiries and requests.
2. **Service Improvement**: To improve the quality of services offered by the company.
3. **Marketing**: To send promotional materials and information about new services, provided that the user has given their consent.

**Article 3: Types of Personal Data Collected**

The types of personal data collected may include:

1. **Identification Data**: Name, surname, email address, phone number, etc.
2. **Usage Data**: Information about how the services are accessed and used.
3. **Cookies**: Data collected through cookies and similar tracking technologies.

**Article 4: Legal Basis for Processing**

The legal basis for processing personal data includes:

1. **Consent**: The user has given explicit consent for the processing of their personal data for one or more specific purposes.
2. **Contractual Necessity**: Processing is necessary for the performance of a contract to which the user is a party.
3. **Legal Obligation**: Processing is necessary for compliance with a legal obligation to which the company is subject.

**Article 5: Data Retention Period**

Personal data will be retained for as long as necessary to fulfill the purposes for which it was collected, including for the purposes of satisfying any legal, accounting, or reporting requirements.

**Article 6: User Rights**

Users have the following rights regarding their personal data:

1. **Right to Access**: Users have the right to request copies of their personal data.
2. **Right to Rectification**: Users have the right to request that the company correct any information they believe is inaccurate.
3. **Right to Erasure**: Users have the right to request that the company erase their personal data, under certain conditions.
4. **Right to Restrict Processing**: Users have the right to request that the company restrict the processing of their personal data, under certain conditions.
5. **Right to Data Portability**: Users have the right to request that the company transfer the data that we have collected to another organization, or directly to the user, under certain conditions.

**Article 7: Changes to the Privacy Policy**

The company reserves the right to update this Privacy Policy at any time. Users will be notified of any changes through appropriate means.

**Article 8: Contact Information**

For any questions regarding this Privacy Policy or the processing of personal data, users can contact the company at the following email address: [email@example.com].

**Effective Date: 07/12/2022**
